PAGE: snapshot popup renders one input per ACTIVE account (role chip on ops/vault; save requires both); Reserves board = dynamic reserve accounts; Ops/Vault cards + trend resolve via roles; EDIT PLAN gains THE ACCOUNTS section (rename inline / role select / ✕ REMOVE = deactivate, history preserved / + ADD ACCOUNT). His repurposing path: rename 'Trailer holding'→whatever + role vault, retire the old Vault row — no code. Marge NOW READS snapshot_balances JOIN plan_accounts (breaking change for her queries — flagged). Tests 677.

- **2026-08-16 · DieStamp contrast (fix, v1.183.2).** Jason: the DELIVERED stamp on load detail needs more contrast. Root cause: the S8 deboss design made the TEXT the dark carve (delivered fg #0c2417 near-black-green on the #2c3549 plate, legibility hanging on a 1px light edge). Inverted the trick: verdict now BRIGHT status color (delivered #6fd08c / paid #7ab0e8 / cancelled+tonu #f08a8a), carved look via dark top text-shadow + faint light bottom edge, glow bumped. EDGE map deleted. DieStamp is shared (LoadDetailPage only consumer today) so any future stamp site inherits. DESIGN LESSON: deboss-by-dark-text on dark metal never reads — carve with shadow, not with the glyph color.

- **2026-08-16 · Trailer semantics corrected (fix, v1.183.1).** Jason's catch on v1.183.0: the snapshot's `trailer` column is his trailer HOLDING account (trailer revenue in → note $230 + loan guarantor out, ZEROES MONTHLY; 55% of trailer revenue committed to vault monthly) — NOT the trade-up fund. The trade-up fund is "any money over and above the vault cushion when I get to that stage" = VAULT OVERFLOW. FIX: 'trailer'-kind stages are now OVERFLOW FUNDS — done when vault − ratchet ≥ target_lo, progress = above/target, currentValue = the above-ratchet amount; the snapshot's trailer column drives NO stage (test pins holding-account invariance: trailer 0 vs 99,999 → identical waterfall). stageDone/stageProgress take the running ratchet (grows only via earlier completed vault stages). UI: snapshot field 'Trailer holding', Reserves board → 3 cells (+ Trailer holding · zeroes monthly), editor kind label 'trade-up fund · vault overflow above the ratchet', waterfall caption 'above the ratchet · of $15–18k', Guide reworded. DB unchanged (kind value 'trailer' kept, column name kept — semantics live in the lib, commented). Tests 677.

- **2026-08-16 · Account Status — the plan tracker (feat, v1.183.0, PR #434).** Jason's full spec + two mockup rounds. THE FRAMEWORK: `plans` (year/label/float lines/weekly reserves/active — one active at a time, POST/PATCH auto-retire others) + `plan_stages` (the waterfall as ORDERED DATA: kind vault|obligation|trailer, obligation stages BIND to obligations — Best Egg bound live at seed, IRS ships unbound until he adds the obligation) + `account_snapshots` (append-only Friday history). Migration 060 dev+prod, RLS all three, seeded THE 2027 PLAN (float 10k/home 13-14k, maint 500/wk, tax 350/wk; stages IRS→floor 15k→Best Egg→trade fund 15-18k→cushion 30-35k). THE CASCADE (his refinement): vault stages RATCHET the protected level; overflow = vault − highest completed vault threshold funds the active non-vault stage ("anything above the floor starts contributing to Best Egg"). RITUAL DECISION (his question, my call he accepted): snapshot FIRST (raw balances) → the page issues orders (SWEEP $X / send overflow) → next snapshot confirms. lib/metrics/planStatus.ts pure + plan-as-input (2028 = data): getSweep/getCushionProgress/getWaterfallStage/getPlanStatus — 15 tests incl. his exact 16,200→$1,200-overflow case, unbound-obligation ghosting, full-done finish line. PAGE /status: plan sentence (ON PLAN/BELOW FLOAT/NO SNAPSHOT stamps + orders), Ops card (SWEEP action), Vault card (fixed non-overlapping legend), waterfall board (done/active/pending + overflow line + bind nudge), reserves, Recharts trend WITH $-Y/date-X axes (his ask), snapshot popup (live verdict as you type), plan editor popup (thresholds + stage rows ▲▼/add/delete/bind + WRITE THE 2028 PLAN = copy-forward + auto-active). ADMIN-ONLY (ADMIN_ONLY_PREFIXES + sidebar adminOnly 'Plan'). Marge reads plans/stages/snapshots/obligations raw. Guide section added. Tests 675.

- **2026-08-16 · The identity chimera (fix, v1.182.5).** Jason's repro: on Brandie's dispatch board → clicked through → landed on HIS admin dashboard → browser back → dispatch board with a NO-NAME user → award-pop storm. DIAGNOSIS (four compounding defects, all frontend identity plumbing — #277's delegated-access model is localStorage-only): (1) getRole() DEFAULTS TO "admin" when the role key is missing — fail-open; any partial/clobbered storage promotes the viewer to the owner UI. (2) Identity is NON-REACTIVE and cross-tab shared: every component reads localStorage ad hoc; two tabs + one storage = mid-session identity swaps with no reload (his exact repro shape: a login/logout in one tab reskins the other tab's next navigation). (3) Award-pop seen-stores were DEVICE-GLOBAL (dash.awards.v1 / dash.dispatch.awards.v1, no user key): identity swap → another user's earned set diffs against the old baseline → STORM ✓. (4) getUser(selfId) fails silently → member null → no-name hero. FIXES: main.tsx storage-event listener — any token/user_id/role/display_name change from another tab hard-reloads (converges every tab on the current session; logout elsewhere → guard bounces to login); auth.hasCompleteSession() (valid token AND user_id AND role — tested incl. the chimera case) + ProtectedRoute now fail-CLOSED (clears + /login on any partial session — kills the admin-default path); both pop stores keyed per identity (BASE.userId) with one-time legacy migration to the first identity that reads it (no upgrade re-storm) + skip when no userId. NOTE FOR JASON: role remains a VIEW boundary — her token can reach owner data by design (#277 phase 2); real enforcement = backend role claims, a deliberate future decision. Tests 660.

- **2026-08-15 · Her projected double-count (fix, v1.182.4).** Jason: "is her committed amount being double counted? projected 4500 with one delivered at 2250 and one committed at 2250." CONFIRMED — it was ONE load wearing both hats: getWeekGrossCommitted excludes only cancelled (delivered loads stay in), and the dispatch card sums earned + committed → every delivered load counts twice in projected AND the PaceMeter (ghost stacks ON TOP of filled). The S9 Pulse double-count reborn: the owner card knew the trick (committedAhead = weekBooked − weekEarned; its PaceBar renders committed as an absolute UNDERLAY — correct), the dispatch card hand-rolled the sum without it. FIX: new rateTargets.getWeekGrossPipeline (in-window, status ≠ delivered — the slice that legitimately stacks on earned; comment names the trap) + tests pinning her one-delivered-load case (pipeline 0, projected stays 2250); dispatch card committed → pipeline. getWeekGrossCommitted UNCHANGED (owner underlay semantics intact). LESSON (now twice): any surface computing projected = earned + something must use a something that EXCLUDES earned — grep for new '+ committed' sums in future reviews. Tests 657.

- **2026-08-15 · Her pace card: BOOKED AHEAD (fix, v1.182.3).** Jason: "she has 5220 in booked status, but it says otherwise." Diagnosis: NOT the net-named-gross fns (her card already uses the getWeekGross* twins) and NOT attribution (load 4024146, $4,573 lh + $647 FSC = exactly $5,220, booked_by Brandie ✓). It's the WEEK WINDOW: the load delivers Aug 21 — next pay week (current: Wed Aug 12 → Tue Aug 18) — and committed counts by landing week, so a Saturday booking for next Friday showed nowhere. FIX (additive; week-pace semantics untouched): new rateTargets.getBookedAheadGross(loads, weekEnd) = gross of live (booked/in_transit, undelivered) loads with delivery_date ≥ weekEnd; test pins her exact $5,220 case incl. this-week + already-delivered exclusions. Card shows amber "BOOKED AHEAD · lands beyond this week" cell when > 0. FORK STATED TO JASON: if he'd rather her whole pace grade by BOOKING week (work done) instead of landing week, that's a deliberate metric change to make together — not slipped in. TEST-FILE LESSON ×2: blind `import {` replace hit the vitest import; and the second card-anchor mismatch died pre-write while the shell chain kept going — a bumped version + green build proved NOTHING had applied. Verify the write happened, not just the pipeline exit.

DELETED: TopAgents.tsx, getTopAgentsByRevenue + collectRecentAgents from lib/metrics/dashboard (AgentRevenue + agentGross KEPT — getAgentGrossTable uses them; first cut sliced too deep, restored from HEAD and re-cut surgically). - **2026-09-06 · The settlement feed (feat, v1.197.0).** Landstar's weekly truth lands in dash. Tables: `settlements` (one per statement, UNIQUE(user, period_ending) — a week is NEVER overwritten) + `settlement_lines` (every line, classified, load-linked). Fed by the server's parser: 39/39 vault statements reconcile against their own printed totals AND the net identity (revenue + refunds − deductions = net — empirically proven, so lineValue's additive refunds is fact, not assumption). Surfaces: THE SETTLEMENTS board on CashFlowPage (weekly rows + cumulative-per-load adjustments strip + measured tiles), settlement verification on LoadDetailPage (cumulative across reach-back adjustments; named late fees amber, unexplained deltas red, no-expectation = neutral "unverified" — never a false ✓). Two-week board: fuel = measured 30d from the fuel log (LOCAL day keys; 90d stays on cost-per-mile — two windows, two jobs), deductions = two ex-advance buckets (day ≤ 7 = insurance-heavy first settlement) applied by the deposit's PERIOD_ENDING via depositToPeriodEnding (bucket-by-deposit-date landed the heavy week wrong at month boundaries — review catch). Every feed self-heals older unlinked lines (late-entered loads claim their history; FIX chips clear). Review: 18 findings incl. BLOCKER — the printed per-line Net is the WHOLE LOAD's aggregate; summing it beside its own components double-counted netToDate ($713.58 vs $1,403.27). Assumptions remain fallbacks only. Tests 803.

- **2026-09-22 · The Money Calendar — ADMIN-03 read into the Plan page (feat, v1.231.0, #500).** Jason's SOP ADMIN-03 · Owner distributions (manual, v1.0, effective 2026-09-22) + the distribution-policy numbers file, nodded on Nod Sheet rev 2 (all leans). TWO BEATS. The Friday: snapshot first, then the ACCRUAL — the pay week's miles (Wed→Tue, `payWeeks.ts`: loads' loaded + deadhead by pickup date, pre-filled, editable) × `plans.maintenance_per_mile` (0.32) → Maintenance; a closed pay week accrues ONCE, on the first snapshot after it closes (snapshot stores `miles` + `pay_week_start`; every earlier week counts as accrued). The MONEY DAY: opens when the month's P&L is filed in Expenses (earliest filed month ≥ `plans.first_money_month` = 2026-08-01 with no snapshot `settles_month`), runs any day — never the first Friday (Jason: it's the new month's Friday): tax = `tax_pct` (20) % of pre-tax profit off the top (loss → $0) → the INTERLOCK, three floors filled in order Ops (float line) → Maintenance (`maintenance_floor` 5000, proposed) → Vault (the ladder's FIRST vault rung, 15k) → surplus = ops − accrual − tax − float; under `min_move` (500) nothing moves; every floor holds → split `objective_pct` (70) to the current objective (first not-done rung AFTER the floor rung), the rest household. Vault money above the ratchet still goes WHOLE to the objective. dash orders, Excel logs: the SOP's seven-line entry renders on the form (COPY THE ENTRY); `settles_month` is the only stored fact (unique per user+month → 409). Account roles gained `maintenance` and `tax` (083 sets them by name). Verdicts: BELOW FLOAT — HOLD · FLOORS FIRST · ON PLAN, read AFTER the accrual. Page: money day panel + MONEY DAY · <MONTH> ▸ button, the interlock board (rings, meters, HELD/NEXT/THEN/MET stamps), the ladder stamps FLOOR 3 / CURRENT OBJECTIVE, Reserves as rows (Maintenance carries the accrual + the deadhead flag, Tax the month's move), the trend full-width, EDIT PLAN with the six settings + first money month (type=month) — `maintenance_weekly`/`tax_weekly` retired on the row. Page: THE CASH BOARD (badge, lowest, OPS NOW ▸, ten rows in the sheet's order, this-week day strip), OPS NOW popup with the live "the board said $P — off by ±$G" (future dates refused client+server), Fuel tile with week actuals, Deductions tile "next first-of-month". Plan page: miles pre-filled by odometer with both readings named, the loads' figure beside; `accrualFlagOf` (backwards → loads → odometer). Settings: "Cash lands" select. Guide: "The cash board — three pay weeks" (NAV literal), Plan section's accrual sentence. Dead code gone: `twoWeekLiquidity`, `settlementDatesForBoard`, `depositToPeriodEnding`. Built via a Workflow (two Opus implementers ‖ three-lens review → fixer applied 11 BLOCKER/BUG findings incl. two in the engine); the mock-wording deviations fixed by hand. - **2026-09-30 · The cash board in Jason's frame (fix, v1.232.1, #504).** Two model errors in v1.232.0, caught by Jason on the live page: (1) a load counted as delivered on its planned `delivery_date` regardless of status → "2 loads delivered so far" for an in-transit and a booked load. Now DELIVERED = status delivered/invoiced/paid; in-transit/booked loads with a planned delivery in an OPEN week are EXPECTED (named, counted in the projection, never as delivered — `NetPayCell.inTransit/booked/expectedNet`); a closed week gets nothing from a load that didn't deliver into it. (2) the Thursday cash was framed as the calendar week it lands in ("last week's net pay lands") — Jason: "the last week deposit would be this week's net deposit reflected on my statement… you're showing for last week the previous week's net deposit." His frame: **the deposit belongs to the pay week that earned it.** Now each column = one pay week with its OWN deposit on its own Thursday and its CASH days Thursday → Wednesday (`BoardWeek.cycle`; its accrual on that Friday, payroll, the drafts); four columns: the week before (DONE, cash days behind us, with its checks) · last (CLOSED, cash landing Thursday) · this (OPEN) · next; the separate Net pay row is gone; the day strip = last week's cash days; state `done` when `cycle.end <= today`. Opening of a column = the cycle's first morning, or the balance typed on its eve (the Wed Sep 30 Ops check opens last week's cash). Sheet's worked numbers in this frame: the week before ends on the Sep 30 check; last week 7,309.08 + 4,715 − 1,908 − 637 − 836 = **$8,643 Wed Oct 7**. Guide: "The cash board — pay week by pay week". Verified in the pane on dev (override → $8,643). Tests 1,647 / backend 295.
- **2026-10-01 · The cash board in Jason's own spec — two periods (feat, v1.233.0, #507).** After v1.232.1 Jason re-specified the board in chat ("please don't assume anything; if you have any questions, ask me") and answered three questions: **two columns** — the pay period he's running (Wed→Tue) and the next; **each column is the money the period itself earns against what it carries** (reading B: "whatever loads I run this week… gets on next week's settlement on Wednesday and then gets paid out next week on Thursday"); **Ops** = his latest typed number (OPS NOW or the Friday snapshot) — "any expenses that come out on a date that's on that date or after that date should get subtracted", the bottom carries to the next column; **deposit** = delivered loads − Landstar's cut + accessorials (dash's net) − the fuel **advances** drawn against those loads (per-load CARD PRE-TRIP draws on the statement; typically ~$2,000/wk, lately more; the statement's actual when in, else $2,000 unless the period's fills pass it — `advancesSource` actual|assumed|fills|pace) − the fixed deductions; **fuel** is paid from the card the advance lands on, so fills never come out of Ops; **the card leftover (advance − fills) goes to business checking every MONDAY** (a line dated the Monday inside the period); **payroll** fixed, Friday; **accrual** = the period's own miles by odometer, paid the Friday after it closes; **bills** on their draft days; **no gap line**; money-day moves stay. A previous period's deposit landing on the Thursday inside the current period is added only when it lands strictly AFTER the Ops number ("if you type after it landed, it's already in your number") — an expense dated the same day still comes off. `cashBoard.ts` rewritten (22 tests on Oct 1's real numbers: the Sep 30 statement $4,718.09 with $2,875 of draws, load 1380689 delivered Oct 1, 1572011 in transit, 1667237 booked); page: rows Ops · Last period's deposit · Deposit · Payroll · Accrual · Fuel · the card · Card leftover → Ops · Bills · (Money day) · Ending; OPS NOW popup simplified; Guide "The cash board — this period and the next"; `cash_assumptions.weekly_fuel_advance` set to 2000 on prod (his number) and read as `advanceDefault`. Dropped: the day strip, the Checked row, the four-column cycles. Lesson: build HIS frame from his sentences, ask the forks, and never apply "on or after" to an inflow that's there by morning. **Review fixes before merge (same PR #508):** an Opus review of the diff found the one real hole — an Ops number older than the current period's Wednesday left everything the previous period still owed after it (its bills, payroll, accrual Friday, Monday leftover, a money day) in NO column, overstating the ending by thousands → `PriorLines[] since` walks back every period with days on/after the number and the current column carries the sum as a **Since your number** row. Counting rules by kind: outflows count on/after a CHECK's day but only AFTER a SNAPSHOT's day (the Friday snapshot already holds its payroll and drafts); money that's there by morning (a deposit, the Monday card leftover) counts only strictly after either; Jason's own moves (accrual, money day) on/after either. A closed period with no loads drew nothing (`advancesSource: "none"`, deposit 0, not −$2,172); the assumed advance is max($2,000, actual fills) — the pace only drives the fuel figure (`spentSource` actual once fills ≥ pace); `normalizeSettlementDay` guards the walk (+ `settlementScheduleService` clamps 0–6); the OPS NOW note prints on the Ops cell; both deposits are tappable; labels read the schedule. 30 tests. Verified in the pane with a Sep 25 base staged on dev: since −$4,919, endings $8,447 / $8,856 by hand.
- **2026-11-01 · The truck's spot reads the trips; a gap names its half-logged record (fix, v1.233.1, #509).** Jason: "the scoreboard [Score a Load] is not putting my truck at the right location — it's not looking at trips for last known location. Trips also has unidentified miles." Diagnosis from prod: the last delivered load was 2194005 (Southlake, TX, Oct 13) but trips 11/12/13 (Southlake → Leighton → Madison → Leighton, Oct 13/19/30) came after; `emptyNextAnchor(loads)` — the ONE anchor behind the Scorer's "truck now", the Lanes map's pin, the Foreman's proximity, the capacity pass and the close-out draft's empty line — read loads only, and the Scorer fell back to the backend's `getLastKnownLocation` (which already unions trips, fills and loads by odometer) only when NO load existed. Guide: the Foreman's anchor paragraph + the trips line. Fixed the pre-existing exhaustive-deps warning on the Scorer's destination factor (`deliveryPlace` memo). 10 new tests (1,655). Lesson: when a page says "last known", find the ONE function every page shares and fix it there — the backend had the right union all along, the frontend anchor never asked it.

NEW `marketCall.ts` (`buildTierCall`/`buildMarketCall`): per type (standard | specialized+hazmat), ≥ 5 loads to call, rules in priority: below break-even (under-floor | cut-costs) → between be and floor (protect at the floor) → evidence-disagrees (held firming vs target ≥ 70th pctile of own loads; held softening vs < 45th) → floor..target (firming → raise ONE step to target; softening → protect with cushion; else hold) → ≥ target (firming → strong / ceiling; else hold); evidence = n, below, pctile, disagree. `tierGauge` → per-type rows with n and pctile (null under 8), no tone/suggestion. - **2026-11-01 · Empty miles by the chain, not the gap (fix, v1.236.1).** v1.236.0 read the next load's deadhead as the odometer GAP between the two loads — zero in Jason's book, where every load's start reading is the previous load's end (the chain is continuous), so every reload graded free and the IN yardstick read 0. `emptyMilesTo` now reads the next load's window minus its loaded miles (the empty run sits inside the window by that convention), the gap only when the window holds no excess (a fresh reading at the pickup), the planned field last. Also cleared ExpensesPage's three `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint errors that predated the branch (derived `selected`/`loading`/`windowFull`, same behaviour). Verified in the pane against dev with the real `2026-10-pl.csv`: confirm step Income $16,566 · Cost $18,905, 19 categories, no warning; a doctored total refused before the confirm step; a missing total warned and summed; saved month reads $16,566 in · $18,905 out. Lesson: an upload that can't find what it expects must refuse, not save what it found.

Forecast (`cashflow.ts buildForecast`): net per RUNNING week — Σ net income over the last six CLOSED months ÷ the weeks they ran (planned home weeks from forecast_adjustments taken out) × 52/12 = a full running month; a forecast month earns weeklyNet × its own running weeks; no tax refund on a loss month; the current calendar month is never an actual (`now` param); `Forecast` gains `weeklyNet`, `runningWeeks`, `baselineMonths`. - **2026-11-08 · Nightly backups (v1.255.0, PR #562; dts-server PR #2).** Nod Sheet "Nightly Backups" (all leans nodded). Migration 103 creates the read-only role dash_backup (LOGIN + BYPASSRLS + pg_read_all_data — 57 of 60 public tables carry RLS with no policies, so a plain reader sees zero rows; the password lives only in /etc/dts/dash-db.env on dts-srv, root 0600). backend/db/dump.sh prod|dev dumps schema public (-Fc, --no-owner --no-privileges) to ~/dash-backups, refuses a client older than the server and a dump shorter than the schema, and looks for the env beside itself then in ~/projects/dash/backend (worktrees carry no env). THE RULE (CLAUDE.md): a dump before every psql -f on prod or dev. The server's nightly backup.sh now dumps dash through postgres:17-alpine on the host network (the Supabase host is IPv6-only), verifies 50+ tables, runs restic unlock before each remote (a stale lock on B2 had failed every nightly since Oct 10 behind green drills on the other remote), and the Sunday drill restores dash-latest.dump and prints loads / settlement lines / last migration, checking the other remote's freshness every week.

It is correct and it IS net. What made it read low: load 4115417 (Nov 4) is a **TONU** worth $1,906 net, excluded by `status.ts`'s documented `NEVER_REVENUE` rule ("the shipper decides what a TONU pays, and that shows up on the statement, not in a projection"), and 4493038 ($1,627) was still `booked`. November at net is $7,382 delivered, or $9,288 if a TONU ever counts as income — Jason's call, left as is. Don't re-derive this.

- **2026-11-09 · The Redesign, phase C — the Fleet tab, dashboard 4 of 4 (v1.267.0). PHASE C's DASHBOARD IS COMPLETE.** Nod Sheet "The Fleet Tab" (https://claude.ai/artifact/MFJwQPEwe7Sb3KyNgN8VMg), seven calls nodded on the lean plus one change Jason asked for at the nod. **Jason asked for the headline himself:** "we need a graph to track monthly miles because if my miles are low, it drives my rate up… Is this the best page for this chart?" — then, at the nod, "the chart needs to also say how many miles per each month". He is right about the mechanism: the ladder books at `(true cost ÷ driven miles) ÷ keep`, so miles ARE the denominator of the floor. His 2026 proves it: Jan 2,077 mi → **$8.21/mi floor**; Feb 6,564 → **$2.68**; and Sep (9,882 mi, $23,101 cost) came out at $2.34 a mile while Oct (5,062 mi, $18,905) came out at $3.73 — **October spent $4.2k LESS and cost $1.39 more on every mile.** Four of the ten filed months needed more than the ~$4.34 he books to, every one a thin-mile month; the blended six-month figure hides all of it. **Built:** `lib/metrics/milesHistory.ts` — `milesHistory(loads, trips, periods, obligationsMonthly, keep, now, months)` → one row per calendar month INCLUDING the one in progress (marked `inProgress`, unlike the cost basis which excludes it), each with `driven` / `loaded` / `empty` / `emptyShare` / `filed` / `cost` / `breakEven`. It reads the odometer through the already-exported `monthMiles`, which unions the load and trip spans so an overlapping repositioning trip is never double-counted, and it repeats getCostBasis's cost rule to the character (`cogs + expense + obligationsMonthly`, and a month missing EITHER total is unfiled) so the chart and the ladder can never disagree about what a month cost. `components/dashboard/fleet/MilesChart.tsx` draws it: stacked loaded-over-empty bars scaled to the peak, that month's break-even printed above its own bar and toned against the rate he books to, the booking floor as the one dashed rule, and — his change — **the mile count printed under every month**. Two units, no shared y-axis; a zig-zag line would have needed a second axis in dollars. **DEFECT CAUGHT ON THE PANE CHECK, worth remembering: `monthMiles` returns `total: 0` both for "nothing ran" and for "real freight whose loads carry no usable odometer reading", and the first draft printed "0" for both** — the dev book has a month with 6,332 loaded miles and no odometer, which rendered as "0 driven" and claimed the truck sat still. `MileMonth` now carries `measured` (an odometer span was readable) and `hasActivity` (a delivered load or logged trip fell in the month); the chart prints the number when measured, **"—" when freight ran but the distance is unknown**, and 0 only when nothing was logged at all. Three tests pin it. 14 tests in all, incl. the overlap case and the year boundary. **Also:** both `GaugeDial` speedometers retired (call 5) — the last of the game's instruments, and the component was deleted once its only two mounts went; the Utilization cluster cell folded into the "How hard she runs" bar that actually breaks the percentage down (call 4); "Fuel + shop + note" now says its window out loud (fuel on the last 30 days) (call 7). FleetTab's props are now `{loads, trips, periods, targets}`. **DEVIATION — call 6 (ds2-board → Panel) was NOT built, and the sheet's premise for it was wrong:** `.ds2-board` and `.ds-panel--default` resolve to the identical background, border and 12px radius, and `ds2-board` is used in **48 files**, so Fleet is nowhere near "the last tab with the old surface". Converting 5 of ~53 instances is a visual no-op that adds diff noise and leaves the rest inconsistent; it belongs to the C1–C6 page sweep, in one pass. Gate: production build clean, tsc clean, eslint clean, vitest 153 files / 2,074 (+14), backend 565 untouched; pane at 1180 in both looks. The Loads recon found these surfaces are not mainly a layout problem — they state things they do not know — so C1 became C1a (correctness, this) and C1b (design, next). Nod Sheet "The Loads Page, Corrected" (https://claude.ai/artifact/GvsPmdZiBmdr9s3urGTtxj), eight calls, all on the lean. **In every case the rule already existed and the surface routed around it.** **(1) Cancelled freight counted as gross:** `loadsViewTotals` hand-rolled `load_status !== "tonu"` instead of importing `isNeverRevenue` (= cancelled AND tonu), so 8 cancelled loads — **$27,530 and 4,317 loaded miles** — were summed into the Loads page's gross. Prod: the All view read $347,068 / 84 loads; it now reads $319,538 earned over 85 rows. **(2) The count contradicted the table:** the same function applied a REVENUE rule to the ROW COUNT, so All said "84 loads" above 85 rows and the TONU filter said "0 loads in this view" above a visible row. `loadsViewTotals` now returns `{rows, earning, gross, miles, rpm}` and the line prints both ("139 loads · 136 earning · $549,459 gross" on dev, count == rows exactly). **(3) An erased number box saved as a measured 0:** `handleChange` ran `Number(e.target.value)` and `Number("")` is 0, while `loadScore.ts` treats 0 deadhead as "the truck is already at the shipper — a real answer" and null as "a refusal to grade". So clearing Deadhead Miles turned no-grade into a confident SCRAP/THIN/SOLID/PRIME — **only on loads with NO odometer window, i.e. BOOKED loads, which is exactly when the grade decides whether to take the load.** New `lib/loads/numberField.ts` → `numberFieldValue(raw)` returns null for an emptied box AND for letters, 0 only when 0 was typed; `numberField.test.ts` pins the whole chain end to end (erased → null → `scoreLoad` verdict null; typed 0 → graded). **(4)** `grossSettled` → **`settledNet`** and "Landstar paid X **gross**" → "… net": the value was always the net and was always differenced against `expectedNet`, so the arithmetic was right and only the word was wrong — and it violated Jason's own ruled convention (MONEY_MATH_AUDIT Part 2: settlement = NET). **(5)** `fuel.ts` coalesced a missing deadhead to zero empty miles and undercounted a load's fuel, two inches below a KPI that refuses the same input with "needs the odometer"; it now returns null unless BOTH legs are known (a 0 deadhead still estimates — zero is an answer). **(6)** the Fuel panel's green "actual" chip described the MILES only while price/mpg could still be ASSUMED_*; it now reads the weakest input (`fuelAllMeasured`, reusing the existing `usingRecentFuel`). The detention banner said "your {n}h free" while holding `DEFAULT_DETENTION_FREE_HOURS` = 3; a new `freeHoursKnown` flag makes it say so until the schedule loads. **(7)** three accessorial writes were `try/finally` with no `catch` (a failed POST/PATCH/DELETE cleared state and refreshed, looking like success) and the amount box POSTed `NaN` on letters — all now report through a new `accError`, matching `setClaimFiled`'s existing pattern on the same page. **(8) TWO CONTRACTS — a Rule change line was required in the PR body.** `fuel.ts` added to `noSilentZero`'s FILES (it is a money file and was outside the ratchet that exists to catch exactly this). And a NEW `oneStatusRule.contract.test.ts`: **`status.ts` has always claimed "a contract greps for it" and NO SUCH CONTRACT EXISTED** — that is how the narrower never-revenue set survived. It greps `lib/metrics/*.ts` (skipping comments and the owner file) for load-status spellings of "tonu"/"cancelled", with the **9 pre-existing call sites allowlisted as DEBT, not endorsement** (agent, agentScorecard, apuHours, facilityLedger, foreman ×2, odometer, perDay, relationships); the list can only shrink. Trip statuses (`t.status`) are a different field and are not matched. **Two existing tests encoded the bugs and were rewritten, deliberately:** the GL-104 test asserted "a view of only TONUs is zero loads" (the count lie) and a fuel test asserted "estimates from loaded miles alone when there's no deadhead" (the silent zero). Gate: production build clean, tsc clean, eslint clean, vitest 155 files / 2,093 (+19), backend 565 untouched; pane at 1180 — count == rows on All, and the TONU filter reads "1 load · 0 earning · $0 gross" over its one row. A modal floats above the page, so Loads was right on both tokens and **the majority was the odd set** — the lesson is that a majority is not a standard. (2) Call 5 (drop the duplicate status badge) would have undone a decision the code names as Jason's, 2026-08-09: the header already collapses to one word via `loadStatusWord`, and the two badges render only for booked/in-transit. **Also named, not built:** gross at two precisions is the documented `format.ts` convention (`money` for aggregates, `moneyCents` for line items), "Deadhead (actual)" vs "(planned)" are two distinct labelled facts, and **Loaded + Deadhead each still print twice within one screen** (the KPI strip as headline, the Mileage panel as the ledger that also carries planned deadhead and the odometer) — defensible as headline-plus-breakdown, but it IS visible duplication and Jason may want one of them gone. **Guide (the rule, applied to C1a's debt as well as C1b's):** new section **"The line above the table"** — C1a changed what the Loads totals strip MEANS and documented it nowhere, so the Guide now says the count is the rows on your screen, that "earning" appears only when the two differ, and WHY cancelled/TONU runs are held out ("a cancelled load would quietly drag the rate down as though you had hauled it for nothing"). **SEMVER — a patch, not the minor that was planned:** CLAUDE.md rules additive→minor, corrective→patch, and a design pass that adds no new fact is neither; the precedent is `refactor: consolidate money/rate formatting to shared helpers` at **v1.124.1**. Calling this 1.268.0 would have over-claimed. Checked at 1180 in dark AND light: modal + its Radix selects, the flat rail, the ring marker, the closed grid row, the Guide section. **"On the road" has no in-transit load in the dev seed, so I flipped one to see it — and the revert was BLOCKED by `loads_delivered_needs_readings`, a NOT VALID check constraint that tolerates legacy rows but rejects new writes;** this seed row is delivered with a null `odometer_end`. - **2026-11-09 · Phase C, C2a(i) — the money pages CORRECTED (v1.267.3, a fix patch).** C2 split on the C1 precedent: C2a corrects, C2b redraws. Nod Sheets: *Cash Flow & Plan* (rev 2, nodded, 12 calls) + the drawing *The two money pages*. **Jason found the sharpest defect himself:** *"the forecast ends on Thursday, just before my payroll comes out."* He was right — `cashBoard.ts:740` DOES subtract the Friday payroll, so the amount was correct, but the row was pushed after the sort carrying **`date: c.deposit.date`** (Thursday) and reading *"Ops after this period's deposit lands."* The number was Friday's and the label was Thursday's; reconciling against the bank on Thursday came up a payroll short. The Ending now takes the **last counted line's** date and names it ("after Friday's payroll", "after Nov 10's bills") — not hard-coded to Friday, because a bill on the 10th drafts later — and carries the gap (`this period ends $X under the $Y float`). **The fuel advance, also his question.** He said it wasn't in the forecast; it was — the `Card leftover → Ops` line, `advance − fills`, dated the Monday inside the period — but invisible for three reasons: the label never said "fuel" or "advance", it sat away from the Thu/Fri cluster, and **in his current period it printed $0**. That last one was `Math.max(0, advances - spent)`: his advances are `CARD PRE-TRIP` lumps that ran **$0–$4,000 across twelve statements**, and on four of them the fills ran past the draw (Sep 2 −$100, Sep 9 −$45, Sep 16 −$75, **Nov 4 −$419, the live period**), every one reported as $0 — always in the direction that **overstates Ops**. The leftover is signed now, the label reads *Fuel advance left → Ops* / *Fuel past the advance → from Ops* / *Fuel, no advance drawn → from Ops*, and the row's render guard changed from `advancesSource !== "none"` to also show a non-zero amount, because the ending subtracts it either way and **a line that moves the ending has to be on the screen**. New `runningAdvancePace(settlements, before)` projects the advance from the **last four statements that drew** (null under four, so the typed figure stays the fallback), replacing `max($2,000, fills)` which could never project below $2,000 though a quarter of his statements did. **`fuelFallback` stopped borrowing `weekly_fuel_advance`** — it is the fallback for how much fuel he BURNS while the advance is what Landstar FRONTS ($1,446/wk vs $2,207), so wiring both to one setting made the board assume they were equal AND reported one blank box as two missing inputs naming a "weekly fuel fallback" that exists nowhere. It is `null` now and the fuel line refuses honestly, pointing at the fuel log. **Also:** the fuel tile's headline and range now count the same weeks (it printed `$1,445.84/wk` over `four weeks $0.00–$1,752.22`, the $0 being the home week the headline excluded); the badge says **EVERY DAY CLEARS / A DAY UNDER THE FLOAT — HOLD** so it stops sharing words with the per-period endings it can legitimately disagree with; the **ladder reads `view` not `status`** (line 275 promised "the whole page previews it" and the ladder was the one panel that didn't); a **blank Vault refuses out loud** instead of deleting the whole ladder panel; StatusPage's two bare `.catch(() => {})` now report through `FetchFailed` (a dead request rendered "NO SNAPSHOT YET", a confident claim built from nothing) and the file joined `noSilentZero`'s FILES; the page's `<h1>` finally says **PLAN**, matching the sidebar. **THREE tests encoded the clamp** — that is how it survived — and all three were rewritten deliberately; +4 new for `runningAdvancePace`. **DEVIATION, and the ratchet talked me out of it:** the sheet said to add `Math.max(0,` to `noSilentZero`'s PATTERNS. It catches **41 lines** across the money files and **40 are correct** — a distance to a floor, a percentage clamp, a week count, a parser depth. A ratchet that must allowlist forty right lines to catch one wrong one teaches nothing, so the pattern was rejected and the rule pinned as a test case instead; the reasoning is recorded in the contract file so nobody tries it twice. **Pane check earned its keep:** tsc, build, 2,098 tests and eslint were all green while the page threw `advanceSrc is not defined` — a stale HMR module from a two-step edit, confirmed stale by a hard reload rather than assumed. It also caught a doubled parenthetical (`$1,259 advanced ($1,259 (your last four draws))`) and a double verb in my own refusal copy (`missingWords` appends "isn't set"). Verified on dev at 1180 by temporarily halving the advance lines to see the negative render and deleting a vault balance to see the refusal — **both restored and re-verified**. Gate: build, tsc, eslint clean; vitest 155 files / 2,098 (+4); backend 565/565. **NOT in this PR, sequenced out deliberately: call 6, typed deposits off `localStorage` into Postgres** — it is the only item touching the DB and the backend, needs `dump.sh prod` and two migrations, and belongs in its own reviewable slice as **C2a(ii)**. NEXT: C2a(ii), then C2b the redraw, then the variance tracker (*Does the forecast hold?*, phase 1 the assumption check needs no migration), then C3 Expenses.
- **2026-11-09 · The Ending vs the BANK — `balanceOn` (v1.267.4, a fix patch).** Jason, reading v1.267.3 on prod: *"the payroll is not being deducted from the current Friday. It's rolling over to the next period. So it's not showing an honest ending amount."* **His ORIGINAL complaint was fixed in v1.267.3** — each column's ending now falls after that period's own payroll — but the fix dated the Ending at its last counted line and that exposed an adjacent, older problem. **The two periods OVERLAP in time.** A period's accrual pays the Friday AFTER it closes, which lands inside the next period's week — and that Friday is also the next period's payroll day. So the current column's Ending is dated Fri Nov 13, counts its own accrual, and sits directly across from the next column's Fri Nov 13 payroll **without counting it**. Nothing was miscomputed: a column's `ending` is that PERIOD's economics (what it earns against what it carries) and the chain is correct — col1 opens on col0's ending and the arithmetic ties. But it is **not a bank balance on that date**, and a reader comparing it to the payroll opposite will correctly conclude it is overstated. **The decisive evidence: the board already had the right model and the Ending was the only thing not using it.** `lowestDay` walks *"every counted line of both columns on its own day, in order, run from the Ops number"* — a true cross-column dated running balance, and it is what the verdict badge reads. So the moves walk was extracted to `datedMoves(columns)` (shared, so the verdict and the bank figure can never diverge) and a new **`balanceOn(base, columns, date)`** returns what the account actually holds at the end of a day. The Ending's note now says it when the two differ: *"the next period has already taken $2,220 by this day, so the bank reads −$4,208"*. **"by this day", not "on this day" — and a test proved the wording wrong before it shipped:** the gap on the fixture is **$2,048, not the $1,908 payroll**, because the next period's week opens Oct 7 and its Canva ($20), Claude ($100) and Analysis Ch ($20) have gone too. I had asserted 1908, the test failed, and the copy was corrected along with it. A second test assumption also died — I asserted the balance falls across the window when on that fixture it rises — replaced with the real invariant: **walked to the far end, `balanceOn` IS the next column's ending, because the two columns are one timeline.** 5 tests. **DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT, and worth its own item: a running-balance column on every ledger row.** That is the standard answer to "what will the bank say" and it would make the Ending nearly redundant — but it restructures the board, which C2b explicitly said it would not touch. Gate: build, tsc, eslint clean; vitest 155 files / 2,103 (+5); backend 565/565; pane-checked at 1180 on dev. **Lesson worth keeping: a figure can be arithmetically correct, chain correctly into the next one, and still be the wrong answer to the question the reader is asking of it.**
- **2026-11-09 · THE ENDING IS THE BANK (v1.268.0).** Third pass at the same complaint, and the first one that gave Jason the number instead of an explanation. His framing is the thing that unlocked it: *"there's two timelines. There's the pay period timeline of how we tally up what is due as far as income goes and deductions from my settlement, as well as the fuel and the advance. And then there's the regular calendar timeline … I get paid on Thursday from my settlement. Friday, payroll comes out. So my true balance is the pay that I make on Thursday minus the payroll."* **He is right and the distinction is load-bearing.** The deposit, the fuel and the advance belong to the **pay period** that earned them — that is what the columns group, and it stays. Payroll and bills are **calendar** events that happen on their day whatever period it is. **The bank only keeps the calendar one.** Because a period's accrual pays the Friday AFTER it closes, the current column's last day falls inside the next period's week — the same Friday its payroll leaves — so the old ending counted its own accrual and not that payroll. v1.267.4 footnoted the gap; he asked twice for the number. **Built:** `datedMoves(columns)` extracted from `lowestDay` and shared, `balanceOn(base, columns, date)` = what the account holds at the end of a day across BOTH columns, `absorbedBy(prev, date)`, and `PeriodColumn.endDate` so the ledger and the arithmetic can never disagree about which day the ending speaks for. Each ending is now `balanceOn(its endDate)`; the next column opens on it and its already-taken lines render **greyed with "already off in the current period's ending"** — the existing "in your number" idiom — so nothing is subtracted twice and he still sees the payroll he is owed sight of. On dev: ending −$1,989 → **−$4,208**, the $2,220 being the next period's payroll plus its early bills; the chain still walks to the same far-end −$2,843. **`cashToday.cashAsOf` had the SAME blind spot** (it walked `board.columns[0]` only) and was fixed with it — harmless for *today*, since today always sits inside the current column, but wrong for any later date and it would have put the Pulse plate and the Cash Flow page on different answers. **RULE CHANGE, `cockpit.contract.test.ts`:** its pinned figure moved 13,829.56 → 12,529.56 and **two assertions that reconstructed the ending from `columns[0]`'s lines alone** were rewritten against a shared `netAfter(from,to)` that walks every column. The rule the file protects — one input, one builder, both surfaces the same number — is unchanged and now holds on *every* date rather than only today. **Nine tests rewritten in all** (six `ending` sums gained a named `HANDOFF = 1908+100+20+20`; three contract assertions), and one I wrote was deleted for being tautological (`ownSum` derived from the value it checked). **Lesson: a figure can be arithmetically correct, chain correctly into the next one, and still answer a question nobody asked.** The semver is a deliberate **minor, not a patch**: the arithmetic was not broken, the *meaning* of a displayed number changed across two surfaces, and a reader scanning the log should be made to look twice. NEXT: C2a(ii) deposits→Postgres, C2b the redraw, the home-time forecast (sheet nodded), the variance tracker.
- **2026-11-09 · C2a(ii) — the typed deposit moves off localStorage (v1.269.0, a feat).** The last of C2a, sequenced out of v1.267.3 because it is the only item touching the DB and the backend. Call 6 of the *Cash Flow & Plan* sheet. **What was wrong:** tapping a deposit on the cash board and typing the figure Landstar has told you wrote it to `localStorage` under `dash.cashboard.deposit-overrides` — on ONE browser. Typed on the laptop, invisible on the phone; cleared site data, gone; **Brandie never saw it**; no backup ever held it; while every other number on that page and the Plan page went to Postgres. **Built:** migration **105 `deposit_overrides`** (user_id + statement_date PK, numeric(12,2), RLS enabled per the house rule) — **keyed by the STATEMENT date (the Wednesday the period settles), not the Thursday the cash lands**, because that is what the board keys a deposit by. `getDepositOverrides` / `setDepositOverride` / `deleteDepositOverride` in `cashflowServices.js` + three routes on `/cashflow/deposit-overrides`, scoped to `req.user.user_id` (= the account owner) like the rest of the file, so a dispatcher sees what the owner typed. Frontend service in `cashflowService.ts` (coerces the numeric string), `readDepositOverrides` replaced by **`depositOverridesOf(rows)`**, and both readers wired: `useCashBoard` (the Pulse plate) and `CashFlowPage`. The page **writes through optimistically** and **puts the old value back on a failure**, with a `FetchFailed` banner saying the board is on its own projection — a figure left on screen that the server never took would be worse than no figure. **Validation that could not exist before:** a statement_date must be YYYY-MM-DD and an amount must be **zero or more** — zero is real (a home week's statement nets nothing) but negative is not, since Landstar carries a negative statement into the next one rather than taking money back out. 3 backend tests (568 total). **Dumps first, both environments** (`dash-prod-20261109-2301.dump` 60 tables, `dash-dev-...` 57) per the Nov 8 rule, then migration applied to **dev AND prod**, both verified `relrowsecurity = true`. **Verified end to end on dev, not just typechecked:** PUT → the board renders *"statement Nov 18 · you said $2,500 … +$2,500.00 ✕ YOU SAID"* with **`localStorage` deliberately emptied**, proving the figure came from the server; DELETE removes it; both validations refuse with their sentence (400). Dev left with no rows. **Trap that cost twenty minutes and is worth remembering: `node --env-file=` is NOT interchangeable with the project's `npx dotenv -e` here** — the worktree backend started that way rejected every token with "Invalid or expired token" (401) even though both loaders hash JWT_SECRET identically when probed directly. Run the worktree's server through the project's own loader instead: `cd ~/projects/dash/backend && npx dotenv -e .env.development -- node ~/projects/dash-pr1/backend/src/server.js`. Also: a `fetch('/api/...')` from the dev page hits VITE, not the API, and the SPA fallback answers **200 with HTML** — the API base is `http://localhost:3000` with no `/api` prefix. Gate: build, tsc, eslint clean; vitest 155 files / 2,104; backend 568 (+3). NEXT: C2b the redraw, the home-time forecast (sheet nodded), the variance tracker.
- **2026-11-09 · C2b(i) — the Plan page REDRAWN (v1.269.1, a refactor patch).** The design half of C2, split Plan-then-Cash-Flow on the C2a precedent. Drawn and nodded in *The two money pages*. **The finding that made it work: the interlock was on this page three times.** The "OPS — THE FLOAT" card and the "THE VAULT — GENERAL RESERVE" card were interlock rows 1 and 3 at 40px, and the Reserves block was row 2 plus Tax. Proof it lost nothing: after deleting both cards, `opsBal` / `vaultBal` / `maintBal` were still live — all three feed `floorBalanceBefore`, which the floors rows already drew. **Nine stacked blocks became six** (PLAN · the standing · the three floors · the ladder · this Friday · the trend) and that is what bought the ladder its room instead of making it compete. **Built:** the two cards deleted; the interlock retitled **"The three floors — filled in this order before anything is distributed"**; **Tax rides there as a fourth WATCHED line** with an UNNUMBERED marker (a numbered `Ring` would put it in a queue it is not in) and a `RESERVE` stamp; Reserves became **"This Friday"** and lost both balance repeats, keeping only what lives nowhere else — the accrual arithmetic and the money-day clause. **The ladder compacts** (`floorsHold = floorsBoard.every(f => f.met)`): every rung keeps its name and its target, the objective and the rung the next dollar actually reaches stay open, and the rest are ONE LINE until the floors hold, when they all expand on their own. New **`NEXT DOLLAR`** stamp on the rung that genuinely receives next — which while the floors are short is the **floor-3 rung filled by the interlock**, NOT the objective, so the two stamps sit side by side saying different true things. `BOARD_TITLE` now **IS `ds2-label`**; the page used the house primitive zero times while 28 other files share it. **Jason's correction, which this is built around: the Plan page is NOT aspirational** — *"That is what I plan on doing with my surplus. So it helps me delegate money."* The ladder is a destination; compacting is about density, never about hiding it. He nodded the fold himself: *"It instills focus."* **A bug I caught in my own code before it shipped:** `floorsHold` was first written `floorsBoard.length > 0 && every(met)`, which **compacts when there is no interlock to read** — the exact opposite of the comment I had written above it. `every` on an empty list is already `true`, so the honest version is the plain one: with no floors to read we cannot say they are short, and the ladder opens rather than folding his plan away on a figure the page never had. Gate: build, tsc, eslint clean; vitest 155 files / 2,104; pane-checked at 1180 in both looks — rungs 3/4/5 compact to one line with names and targets intact, every request 200 (the 401/404 in the console buffer were my own earlier curl tests, confirmed stale against the network log). NEXT: **C2b(ii) Cash Flow** (12 heading idioms → `ds2-label`, the three wells lifted out of THE SETTLEMENTS with their windows stated, one money format, the hidden weeks-off, per-panel skeletons, the 💰), then the home-time forecast, then the variance tracker.
- **2026-11-09 · Brandie's board REBUILT — now · next · owed (v1.270.0, a feat).** A detour Jason called: *"That needs to be redone. It needs to be items that are relevant to what a dispatcher needs to see."* Nod Sheet *Brandie's board*, all leans nodded. **The state that made the case, read off prod:** one load rolling (4493038 Ennis TX → Odessa TX, delivering 08:00 the next morning) and **nothing booked behind it** — so the truck would sit empty in Odessa — while her dashboard rendered that as **"Booked · 0 · on the board"**, one of five equal KPI cells, between "Loads · MTD" and "Deadhead · MTD". **TABS: NO, and that is the answer to his question.** The owner's five tabs earn their keep because he has five questions on five horizons and opens the page to THINK. She has ONE question that repeats on a horizon of hours — is the truck covered, what next — and opens it to ACT. Her page did not suffer from too much on one screen; it was **ordered by what dash can compute rather than by when she needs it**. **Built:** `lib/metrics/dispatchBoard.ts` — `dispatchBoard(loads, freeHours, today)` → `{now, gap, owed}`; the truck frees on the **LAST** load to deliver (not the first), an undated load never sets the moment and never blocks one that can, `sittingNow` when nothing rolls and nothing is booked. 14 tests incl. the adverse cases. Three bands that **change colour with the state** — NOW amber when something rolls, **NEXT RED when nothing is booked behind it**, OWED quiet — because a covered week and an empty one should not look the same at a glance. NOW names each rolling load, where it is going and when it delivers in the words that matter (`whenWord`: today / tomorrow / the weekday), and **flags a rolling load with no `shipper_in` in red — that is not a data-entry nicety, it is money that cannot be claimed because the detention clock was never started**. NEXT hands straight to The Foreman and Score a Load with the booking floor under it. OWED is detention to claim + pickups inside three days with no window + missing in-times + Integrity rows. **Removed, and the engine with it:** `DispatchAgentsTable` ranked agents by gross with **🥇🥈🥉 medals and a Trophy** directly above `DeskLeaderboardPanel` doing the same ranking better — **two leaderboards on one page, and the second was MINE from v1.266.0**. The table, its only engine `getAgentGrossTable` and its 2 tests all went; leaving the engine behind is exactly the mistake phase B made with `agentLeaderboard`. Her pace meter and quarter-weeks moved to **`/my-recap`**, which already does month/quarter/year properly — a worklist should not compete with a report card. That took a dozen derivations with it (deadhead trend, load-count delta, week earned/committed, gauge geometry). **Pane-checked AS A DISPATCHER without creating a user:** `isDispatcher()` reads `localStorage.role` only (a frontend view boundary, #277 Phase 2), so setting `role=dispatcher` with the owner's token renders her board and touches no data. Dev has no delegate; prod has Brandie (role dispatcher, delegate, since Jul 16, 14 loads booked). Saw both states — empty (red "Empty now — every hour is an empty hour") and populated (rolling load + in-time flag + "1 booked behind it", NEXT correctly dropping its red). **A lapse worth keeping: to see the populated state I flipped two dev loads and nulled a `shipper_in` WITHOUT capturing it first.** Recovered it exactly from the morning's `dash-dev-20261109-2301.dump` by reading the dump's own `COPY public.loads (…)` header for the column order — positional guessing was wrong, the COPY order differs from `ordinal_position`. Both rows verified back to the dump's values. **Capture before you overwrite; the dump is the reason that was recoverable.** Also met `loads_delivery_after_pickup`, which refuses a delivery before its pickup. Gate: build, tsc, eslint clean; vitest 156 files / 2,116 (+14 new, −2 with the dead engine). NEXT: **the phone ping (location option B — `truck_pings`, a POST route and an iOS Shortcut; option A, the free-at line, shipped inside this)**, then C2b(ii) Cash Flow, the home-time forecast, the variance tracker.
